**48. ***{the word that I have spoken}* The doctrine of Christ, the Gospel, which the wicked now so securely contemn, shall once rise in judgment against them, and condemn them, (see Mark 16:16; John 3:18) by so much more heavily, by how much greater means of salvation they have neglected.

**49. ***{what I should say}* God therefore anointed him, Isa. 61:1. So that whatever he taught concerning faith, justification by it, rewards, punishments, the resurrection, baptism, &c., was the eternal counsel of God revealed by Christ, of all which he changed nothing in the delivery thereof.

**50. ***{I know that his commandment is life}* That is, that the doctrine of the Gospel which I have, at the command of my Father, preached unto you, is the word of life (as Peter confessed, ch. 6:68), that which truly shows you the only way and means to eternal life, if you believe and obey it. And so he aggravates their unbelief, who were so blind and malicious in their own wrong.

## CHAPTER 13

**1. ***{Now before the feast of Passover}* See Matt. 26:1; Mark 14:1; Luke 22:1. See Is. Casaubon, *Exercit. ad Annal.*, 16, N. 28.

*{he loved them unto the end}* Not only his disciples, but all his elect, which are here called his own; those which his Father gave him. See John 17:20. He loved them even to the death, which he suffered for them; and so, incessantly; and when he saw the danger coming towards him, he took the more care for them.

**2. ***{supper being ended}* Or *when they were at supper*; a little before the institution of the Lord’s Supper, Matt. 26:26. That is, after the supper (wherein according to the ceremonial law, they ate the paschal lamb) was ended, before they had taken away the remainder of bread and wine, see Luke 22:19, 20.

**3. ***{given all things into his hand}* See c. 3:35. This is said, to set off his admirable humility, to which for our sakes he descended, who knew and remembered the power which God had given him: the meaning is, as if it were said, although he well knew that the Father had given all things into his hands (as Matt. 28:18) and that he was now shortly to ascend into that incomprehensible glory, which as God, he had eternally with the Father, yet as man, he would thus perform the part of a servant, in washing his disciples’ feet.

**4. ***{riseth from supper, &c.}* Leaving his disciples sitting; lays aside his garments (which they used to wear, long, and loose) *τὰ* *ἱμάτια*, Hebr. *שמלה* *shimlah*, Gen. 9:23, which the 70 give thereby *τὸ* *ἱμάτιον*.

**5. ***{began to wash his disciples’ feet}* Partly to show his admirable love to them, to give them an example of humility, and love one to another; and partly to intimate, that he only cleanses us from our sins, as 1 John 1:7.

**6. ***{Lord dost thou wash my feet?}* This he seems to say as fearing it had been too much dishonor for Christ the Son of the immortal God, to wash a sinner’s feet (see Luke 5:8), not then fully understanding (as after he did, 1 Pet. 1:19) that he came to redeem us, *with his own precious blood*: in effect he says; O my Lord, should you wash my feet with those sacred hands, with which you opened the eyes of the blind, with which you did exercise divine power in healing the sick, raising the dead, &c. I am unworthy to perform this office to you, and would you do it for me?

**7. ***{what I do thou knowest not now}* Hereby Christ signified, that he now did something more than simply wash their feet: there was in his action, a teaching mystery, which at present they knew not, until they were further instructed, they yet perceived not, that it was not so much material for them to have clean feet, as to be cleansed by the blood of Christ, and to have true humility and love to one another.

**8. ***{I wash thee not}* And make you clean from your sins, freely pardoning them, and sanctifying you.

*{thou hast no part with me}* So the Hebrews express an alienation, or disclaim of that which we call right, title or interest. So Deut. 14:27, it is said of the Levite *אין* *לו* *חלק* *ונחלה* *עמך* *en lo chelek vena chala himmach*—70. *οὐκ* *ἔστιν* *αὐτῷ* *μερὶς* *οὐδὲ* *κλῆρος* *μετὰ* *σοῦ*, *he hath no part nor inheritance with thee*. So Deut. 18:1. See 1 Kings 12:16; 2 Sam. 20:1. Optatus, l. 5, says hereon, *negat illi Christus regnum, nisi accepisset obsequium*. Christ denied him the kingdom except he had accepted the obsequy.

**9. ***{Lord, not only my feet}* Either terrified with the threats or now better understanding the mystery, he wholly subjects to Christ’s will: as if he said, if it be of that consequence; if you did mean, washing from my sins, wash me thoroughly: as Ps. 51:2. See also 1 John 1:7; 1 Cor. 6:11; Titus 3:5, 6.

**10. ***{needeth not}* That is, to be purged, save only to the sense of the sinner’s own conscience, by daily renewing of repentance for the remainder of those corruptions which defile him. Optatus forecited, lib. 5, understands this washing, of baptism, which may not be iterated: *Absit* (says he) *ut jam lotum revocemus ad fontem—absit ut iteremus quod semel est; aut duplicemus quod unum est*. God forbid that we should recall him who is already washed to the font—that we should iterate that which is (but) once, or should make double that which is but one; the apostle saying, there is *one Lord, one faith, one baptism*, Eph. 4:5. If (says he) you give another baptism, *date alteram fidem*, give another faith: if you give another faith, give also another Christ, another God.

*{ye are clean}* Your sins are remitted and done away; not that there shall no sin remain in you henceforth; but because none shall be imputed (so ch. 15:3), you are regenerate, and in part sanctified.

*{but not all}* Not all of you, not absolutely any of you; Judas must be accepted in their company; and the remainder of the old Adam in the best of their persons: a Canaanite left to exercise, not to overcome, nor condemn them that are in Christ, Rom. 8:1.

**11. ***{who should betray him}* *τὸν* *παραδιδόντα*, who it was that now agitated that treason: therefore he said to him v. 27, *that which thou doest, do quickly*.

**12. ***{after he had washed their feet}* The evangelist leaves it to be understood, that after the contest with Peter, he washed both Peter’s feet, and the rest of the apostles, without further contradiction.

*{taken his garments}* After he had put on his garment, which he had laid by, v. 4.

*{sat down again}* *ἀναπεσὼν* *πάλιν*, falling, or lying down again according to their manner of eating; to invite his disciples to a new supper, spiritual, and mystical, the truth, which the former supper represented, as a forerunning shadow of that which more nearly represents his church with his body and blood; not only as the fathers (*who fed on him to come*, 1 Cor. 10:3, 4) but as his body the church, unto the world’s end, which in the sacrament of the Supper here instituted, feed on his body and blood (by faith in the representing mystery) once offered on the altar of the cross, and therein fully satisfying the justice of God, and accomplishing the redemption of all the elect.

*{Know ye what}* That is, as he said to Peter, v. 7, you do not yet know, but now will I teach you; this he speaks unto them, yet ignorant what he meant by this his act of humility, that they might be more attentive to, and retentive of that he would thereby teach them: as if he had said, now mark well and remember what I have done in washing your feet, that you may know how you ought to imitate my love and humility.

**13. ***{Master and Lord}* *διδάσκαλος* *καὶ* *ὁ* *κύριος*, Master, or Teacher and Lord: the Syriac words here used, *רבן* *rabban*, and *מרן* *maran*, (as in *Maranatha*, the Lord comes, 1 Cor. 16:22) sound something more sacred and divine, than ordinarily *διδάσκαλος* and *κύριος*. So that he now takes up an argument from their own mouths, to show what they ought to do in imitation of him, except they will contradict themselves and in actions dissent from their own words of common use.

**14. ***{Ye also ought to wash}* That is, if there be need, and mutual edification require it (as 1 Cor. 6:19), you ought to be ready, and willing to serve one another in love, Gal. 5:13, and to this he urges his own example; as also Matt. 11:29. So that here, *καὶ* *ὑμεῖς*, *and you*, is put for *πολλῷ* *μᾶλλον* *καὶ* *ὑμεῖς*, *much more ought ye*, seeing you are not (as you confess me) lords over one another, but fellow servants, equally bound in mutual duty: for as Optatus says, lib. 5, *est universis servientibus non dominium, sed ministerium*: all servants have belonging to them, ministration not dominion.

**15. ***{given you example}* See on v. 13. Example is the powerful rhetoric: *prius disce qui doces*, Lactant., lib. 4, cap. 23, first learn you who teach: men had rather have examples, than words: it is easy to speak, but hard to perform: would we had as many good doers, as speakers: the perfect teacher ought both *dicere praecipiendo*, and *confirmare faciendo*, ib. c. 24, speak in giving precepts, and confirm the same in his own practice.

**16. ***{servant is not greater, &c.}* Ch. 15:20; Matt. 10:24; Luke 6:40. Much less is he that serves than his master, much less the ambassador, than his prince that sends him, though he represent his person for a time in that commission, as you shall do mine in your preaching, see 2 Cor. 5:20, and compare therewith Luke 10:16. By this argument from the greater to the lesser, he excites them to do as he had done: Do not you scorn, or think yourselves too good to do any office of holy love one for another, seeing I, whom you profess your Master, and the Son of the living God, have vouchsafed therein, not only by precept, but also by my own example, to teach you.

**17. ***{if ye do them}* If you be indeed ready to serve one another in love (which is the sum of the law, Rom. 13:9) and humility, which is the ground of religion, *Not the hearers of the Law are just before God, but the doers of the Law shall be justified*, Rom. 2:13; Rom. 10:5, &c. The cherubims, Ezek. 1:8, had hands under their wings: the one might signify knowledge of God and his will, (the other action) which we also must know that we may obey him, and in obedience do, that we may be happy: otherwise *to him that knoweth to do good, and doth it not, to him it is sin*.

**18. ***{I know whom I have chosen}* 2 Tim. 2:19. I know whom I have chosen to eternal life, and also whom I have chosen to be my apostles, Luke 6:13. I knew what they had been, were, and would be: I did not ignorantly choose the traitor and son of perdition, to the charge of the bag, that he also might for a time, be one of my domestics and apostles, it was that the Scripture might be fulfilled, that is, the will of God declared in the Scripture, it was the determinate counsel of God, that one of his own should betray him; Acts 4:28.

*{he that eateth bread with me}* Ps. 41:9, 10.

*{lift up his heel against me}* Has endeavored to tread me underfoot. See Ps. 41:10. So that by lifting up the heel, is meant to hurt by treachery: it is a metaphor derived from beasts, which suddenly turn heels against their masters when they have full fed them. See Deut. 32:15.

**19. ***{now}* *ἀπ*’ *ἄρτι*, *from henceforth*.

*{I tell you before}* How one of you shall betray me, lest when you see it come to pass, you should startle, and your faith in me should be too much shaken: as if so be I had been mistaken and deceived in my choice, and so not God omniscient: no, no, God cannot be deceived: on which ground Augustine proves, that none of the elect can finally fall away.

*{that I am he}* That is, the Christ whom the Scriptures forepromised, and the ceremonies of the law prefigured.

**20. ***{Verily, verily, I say, &c.}* Matt. 10:40; Luke 10:16. Because he had before taught his disciples humility by precept and example, and that he should be betrayed by one of his own, lest they should be too much troubled to think that when Christ should thus be taken from them, they should be hated of all men as traitors, or contemptible as submitting themselves, as Christ commanded them to the meanest offices of mutual love, here he proposes a great reward to those that should receive them, and show any kindness, mercy and goodness to them, and that he would take it on his own account, and as done to himself.

**21. ***{When Jesus had thus said}* Matt. 26:21; John 11:33 and 12:27. He was troubled by the concourse of several passions, fear, sorrow and indignation at Judas’ odious treason.

**22. ***{looked one on another}* Being troubled and very anxious, because of Jesus’ speech. See Matt. 26:21, 22; Mark 14:19. They not yet knowing which of them he meant.

**23. ***{leaning on Jesus’ bosom}* Which by reason of their posture at meals, he had convenience to do. See Luke 7:38.

*{whom Jesus loved}* That is more tenderly than the rest, though he loved all his, v. 1.

**24. ***{beckoned to him}* *Νεύει*, *nodded the head*; giving thereby a silent sign, that he would desire him to ask Jesus whom he meant, he having convenience by reason of his nearness to ask privately.

**25. ***{then lying on Jesus’ breast}* That is, leaning nearer to Jesus’ breast, that he might tell him in his ear, which of them was the traitor; so probably he whispered unto Jesus, and Jesus answered him that the others might not hear.

**26. ***{I shall give a sop}* Or *morsel of bread*. Cyril notes that he gave no other sign whereby to describe the traitor, than that which the prophet gave, Ps. 41:9, 10, and Chrysostom’s conjecture was not improbable, (that Christ gave this sign to remember him of the table and bread, whereof he did partake, that he might not betray him, and rage against his feeder) if Christ had spoken openly, that all might hear: the disciples could not have so doubted as we see, v. 28.

**27. ***{Satan entered into him}* And took possession of him: not that the morsel hurt him; but because that signal, conscious Judas perceived, or feared that his treason was discovered; therefore being embittered and vexed, he resolved to prosecute it with speed and effect.

*{do quickly}* He permits only, he encourages not the traitor: he knew his hour was come, and desired to accomplish the work of man’s redemption, for which he showed himself ready: and hereby he cast him out of his family, whom Satan possessed.

**28. ***{no man at the table}* Except John, into whose ear Christ had revealed it, v. 26.

**29. ***{Judas had the bag}* See on ch. 12:6.

*{give something to the poor}* Thus they conjectured; because though his stock was but slender, yet they knew he used to spare something to the poor, that he might therein leave us an example.

**30. ***{and it was night}* It might have seemed unseasonable for the traitor then to repair to the high priests, not to discover, but to design a treason: but no importunity hinders ardent malice: and thus also we see that word is true; evil doers, shun the light, ch. 3:20. So when they had taken Christ, he told them, *This is your hour, and the power of darkness*, Luke 22:53. Night best suits with shameful acts, which the prince of darkness loves.

**31. ***{Now is the Son of Man glorified}* That is, he shall shortly be glorified in the admirable work of man’s redemption: for in death to overcome death is, as Chrysostom says well, *Hom.* 71, *δόξα* *μεγάλη*, great glory: this Christ only could do.

**32. ***{if God be glorified in him}* That is, in his obedience to the death, and man’s redemption in his death, then however scandalous and ignominious his sufferings may seem to the world; yet so shall he enter into his true and unspeakable glory. See ch. 21:19; Phil. 2:8, 9; Luke 24:26, teaching us that it is glorious to suffer for the honor of God.

**33. ***{little children}* Because his departing from them by the cross must needs afflict their hearts, therefore he prepares them to patience, by the intimation of his fatherly love and care of them, as of tender children.

*{as I said unto the Jews}* Ch. 7:34.

*{ye cannot come}* You cannot now presently come after me to heaven, as you were wont on earth to follow me, when you missed me.

**34. ***{A new commandment I give you, &c.}* Lev. 19:18. It appears it was an old precept, and the sum of the law, Matt. 22:39, 46; 1 John 3:11, but continually to be renewed, ch. 15:17; 1 John 4:21; Eph. 5:2. He made the pattern new, whereof he says (*as I have loved you*) I did not pay any debt of your merit (says Chrysostom) I began to love you freely, so love you those who have not deserved your love: love is the most certain and undoubted mark of Christ’s servant (v. 35), for it is *πάσης* *ἀρετῆς* *ὑπόθεσις*: the foundation of all virtue. See 1 Cor. 13:1, &c.

**35. ***{By this shall all men know}* By this, or in this, *ἐν* *τούτῳ*, it is an Hebrew manner of expression, Gen. 42:15, *בזאת* *bezoth*, *in this*, 70. *ἐν* *τούτῳ* *φανεῖσθε*. Moses’ disciples are known by their circumcision, &c., others have their marks and habits; but this is the mark by which I will own you, and by which also you shall know yourselves, and the world also may know you to be mine. It is a vain brag of Christianity without love and charity: Satan will permit men any disguises, semblances, or external works of religion, upon condition that he may but set his own image (that is, malice) on them; because he well knows, that all seeming excellencies in the world, without true charity, are nothing. See 1 Cor. 13:1, &c. Charity is the life of religion, by which faith itself works, Gal. 5:6.

**36. ***{Thou canst not follow me now}* Yet you are not ripe for your dissolution: you shall follow me into glory, but yet your course is not yet finished: the saints may desire to be dissolved, and to be with Christ, Phil. 1:23, but their days are numbered, which they must first accomplish.

**37. ***{I will lay down my life for thy sake}* Matt. 26:33; Luke 22:33. He meant it now, and at last did it: but this his example teaches us, not to presume on our own strength; seeing that God only gives constancy in good, and many times permits us to strong temptations to show us our own weakness, and to correct our overdaring confidence in ourselves.

**38. ***{The cock shall not crow, &c.}* See Matt. 26:34 and Luke 22:34.

## CHAPTER 14

**1. ***{Be troubled}* So v. 27; ch. 16:1, which he speaks, not commanding or approving any stoical apathy, as if he would have men put off natural affections, and to be troubled at nothing; but forbidding dejection of mind, and diffidence in afflictions proceeding of unbelief.

*{believe in me also}* In me whom God has appointed to be your peace and reconciliation: they were now to encounter strong temptations, therefore he arms them with consolations, lest the novelty and suddenness of unexpected trials should overcome them.

**2. ***{many mansions}* This whole speech being metaphorical, the meaning is, there is place in the kingdom of heaven for all the elect; (which are many) according to their several degrees of glory assigned them. See on Luke 16:9; Dan. 12:3; 1 Cor. 15:41, 42.

**3. ***{I will come again}* At the latter day, Acts 1:11; Acts 3:21; Acts 17:31. Gr. *πάλιν* *ἔρχομαι*, *I come again*: it is a present for the future; as certain, as if it already were: this may either import the death of every particular, or the universal judgment of all; either of them being instant and most certain; the sense is, though I go from you for a little time: I will neither desert you nor be unmindful of you in these things which shall concern God’s glory, and your salvation, I will ever hear you, and timely relieve you.

**4. ***{Whither I go ye know}* By these often repetitions of his going hence, he intimates, that his death should be only a recess and withdrawing out of this world for a time, into the presence of God in heaven, that we may resolve how we must follow him our Head, and whither: this way he says, they know, because he had declared to them, that by death, he must enter into his glory.

**5. ***{We know not whither thou goest}* That is, but imperfectly know; for they did not yet well understand, nor remember what he had taught them.

**6. ***{I am the way}* *ἡ* *ὁδὸς*, that way, by which only man can enter into eternal blessedness, Acts 4:12. Cyril, *cat.* 10, reads *ἡ* *θύρα*, the door, as John 10:7, to the same sense: as Cyril. Alexandr. here says, *ἅπαντα* *πάλιν* *ἡμῖν* *ἐν* *αὐτῷ*, we have all in him. He only is that way which leads to heaven; the truth prefigured by all the legal ceremonies, as ch. 1:17, the life; the author and giver of eternal life, as ch. 11:25.

**7. ***{from henceforth}* See c. 13:19. Gr. *ἀπ*’ *ἄρτι*, hereafter, after the Holy Ghost the Comforter shall come and *teach you all things*,—v. 26.

*{have seen him}* By seeing the Father, he here means, knowing him in Christ: no man has seen, or can see his essence: but he that believes has seen his mercy.

**8. ***{Shew us the Father}* Show us him, as he was revealed to the prophets, *τοῖς* *σωματικοῖς* *ὀφθαλμοῖς* (as Theophylact expresses it) to our bodily eyes.

**9. ***{Have I been so long with you, &c.}* He taxes their ignorance, who conceived that the knowledge of God was, or could be by sense: know you not me? Who I am by nature? And whence I am? That I am one in essence, and eternal substance with the Father. See ch. 10:38. See also ch. 12:45.

**10. ***{I am in the Father, and the Father in me}* For the fullness of the Deity remains in Christ, Col. 1:19 and 2:9. See Fulgen. *ad Monim.*, lib. 3 (*paulo ante finem*) and Augustin. *Paulin.*, ep. 112, cap. 4, *fine*. The Son is in the Father (says Athanasius, *To.* 1, *Contr. Ar. orat.* 3), *ὡς* *τὸ* *ἀπαύγασμα* *ἐν* *τῷ* *φωτὶ*, *as the splendor in light*: truly in an incomprehensible manner: for Christ’s divine generation, as Fulgentius well says, *object. Ar. discus.*, *inenarrabilis est, ignorabilis non est*: is such as none can declare, nor any be ignorant of.

*{not of myself}* Not of myself as man, not so of myself, as if the same were not of my Father; whatsoever I speak to you, as man to man, God speaks to you by me, Heb. 1:2.

*{he doth the works}* The miracles which you have seen me do, are all wrought by divine power, not human.

**11. ***{for the works’ sake}* If the infallibility of my words prevail not with you, let the excellency of my works and miracles which you have seen me do (works exceeding all power of the creature) persuade you to believe that I am in the Father, and the Father in me; in him *dwelleth all the fullness of the Godhead*,—Col. 2:9.

**12. ***{shall he do also}* He exhorts them not to be offended at his passion, for as much as this power of doing miracles shall be derived to themselves also, whereby it shall appear that he which suffered in the infirmity of his assumed humanity, is also truly God, who alone could give such power to men.

*{and greater works than these}* He seems to mean hereby the conversion of men to the faith by their preaching the Gospel, as Acts 2:41, &c.

*{because I go unto my Father}* Because going up unto the right hand of my Father, I will send the Holy Ghost upon you, by whose power you shall preach, and do miracles to the conversion of men to God, and their salvation: see Eph. 4:8; Ps. 68:18.

**13. ***{whatsoever ye shall ask in my Name}* See Matt. 7:7; 1 John 5:14 and ch. 16:23. If it be according to the will of God, I will do it for you; for the Father has committed all power unto me, Matt. 28:18.

**14. ***{in my Name}* Through my mediation; for my merit’s sake.

*{I will do it}* I will hear and grant your requests, which are to God’s glory: I who am coequal with the Father will do for you.

**15. ***{If ye love me, keep my commandments}* As if he said, sorrow, and sad dejected countenances at the knowledge of my departure hence, are not the arguments of love which I require of you, but faith and obedience: love indeed cannot be fruitless; but *probatio dilectionis exhibitio est operis*, Greg. *hom.* 30, the proof of love is in the exhibition of the work: obedience to Christ shows how men love him. See v. 21.

**16. ***{another Comforter}* Because they could not be without much trouble in an impious world; he promises to send the Holy Ghost to comfort them, and remain with them, to supply his bodily absence, wherein he comforted them during his abode with them in the world.

**17. ***{Spirit of Truth}* Who is truth, because very God, inspiring his with knowledge of the truth, and giving them sweet and comfortable tastes of the graces of sanctification; thus dwells he in them; concerning this it is written, 1 John 2:27, *the anointing which ye have received of him, abideth in you*, &c. See Gal. 2:20.

**18. ***{I will not leave you comfortless}* Or *orphans*, as forsaken orphans, who have none to take care of them; but will come again to you after my resurrection, showing myself to confirm your faith; but specially by inspiring you with the admirable gifts of the Holy Spirit.

**19. ***{yet a little while &c.}* A few hours; for his death drew near: and after that, he would not be seen of any, but his elect witnesses, to whom he showed himself alive from the dead according to the Scriptures, and whereas they might possibly fear, that they must now die with him, he says, *ye shall live also*, to be my witnesses; and you shall believe, and live in me eternally.

**20. ***{and you in me}* As you are lively members of my church, my body of which I am the Head, Col. 1:18, 24; Rom. 12:5, to whom I communicate grace and life, Eph. 1:22; Eph. 4:16.

**21. ***{is he that loveth me}* See on v. 15.

*{I will love him}* Not that he did not first love that man, to whom he gives an heart to love him and keep his commandments; but *ad hoc diligam, ut manifestem*: I will so love, as that I will manifest the same in happy effects, Prosper. Now he loves us, to give us faith and obedience: but in the life to come he will love us in rewarding us: *prorsus donum est Dei, diligere Deum: ipse ut diligeretur dedit, qui non dilectus, dilexit*; it is altogether the gift of God, that we love God; he gave us hearts to love him, who when we loved him not, loved us: see Rom. 5:5, 7, 8, 10.

*{and will manifest myself unto him}* Chapter 1:17. Understand this of a spiritual exhibition of himself, not to a bodily presence, *ad hoc diligam, ut manifestem*, Augustine, *tract.* 75. I will so love him as to manifest myself to him. He now loves us to give us faith to believe, then he will love us so as that we may see him, and in seeing receive the reward of faith: now we love in believing that which we see not, but then we shall love in seeing that which we now believe.

**22. ***{not Iscariot}* Not the traitor Judas, but the same who was called Lebbeus or Thaddeus: Matt. 10:3. See hereof, Jo. Drusius *ad Voc. N.T. Com.* 1, *Thaddaeus & ibid. Lebbeus*.

**23. ***{He will keep my words}* He will obey me: to him will we come, as to our sanctified temple: and bring with us the inestimable treasures of godliness, and true and lasting consolation; we loved him from eternity, and in due time inspired him with the spirit of regeneration, faith, hope, charity, obedience, and we will come unto him, in whom we have begun this work of grace and mercy, by a multiplication of all spiritual gifts, giving him a comfortable perseverance therein; unto eternity.

**24. ***{is not mine}* Not mine as man: it is his that sent me, that is, God’s word. See ch. 6:38 and ch. 7:16 and v. 10.

**25. ***{being yet present with you}* I have spoken these things to your outward ears, and though you perceive not the fruit thereof as yet, you must expect a better increase, when the Holy Ghost the Comforter shall speak to your hearts and understandings, after my ascension into heaven.

**26. ***{the Comforter}* C. 15:26. *παράκλητος*, or the advocate. So Christ is called *παράκλητος*, 1 John 2:1. See Rom. 8:16. They were called *Advocates*, *παράκλητοι*, who were called or retained to speak or plead for them who had indictments against them, or otherwise, were impleaded in any court of judicature. One entreated to favor another in judgment: an intercessor. Philo *de vit. Mos.*, l. 3. See Ulpian., l. 3, *D. de procur.*; Joh. Drus. *praeter.*, l. 4; John 14:16.

*{he shall teach you all things}* He is that good sanctifier of his church, *καὶ* *βοηθὸς*, *καὶ* *διδάσκαλος*—the helper and teacher of his, Cyril, c. 16. If he teach not within, *Doctoris lingua exterius in vacuum laborat*, the teacher’s tongue labors externally in vain. We all hear one voice of a preacher, but what different apprehensions, affections, and resolutions of ill and good concerning the same we have? The word is one and the same, but in some it proves a savor of death, in others a savor of life unto life: the difference is, as this, *Magister interior*, inward Master teaches some, and leaves others to their own obstinacy, and blindness, to pervert all they hear to their own destruction, 2 Pet. 3:16.

*{whatsoever I have said unto you}* For there is but one truth, which Christ taught, and the Holy Ghost remembered them of, and opened unto them. *οὐ* *γὰρ* *ἄλλα* *Χριστοῦ* *διδάγματα*, *καὶ* *ἄλλα* *τὰ* *τοῦ* *πνεύματος*, the doctrine of Christ, and the Holy Ghost, are not divers, but one and the same truth of God. Cyril. *qu. s.*

**27. ***{my peace I give unto you}* He yet continues in the same argument of consolation; according to the custom of friends departing, taking a solemn leave, giving charge, and wishing all prosperity and happy success; all which the Hebrews comprehended in *שלום* *shalom*, Gen. 43:26; 2 Chron. 15:5; Exod. 4:18, &c. But Christ here intimates a peace, which the world can neither give, nor take away, peace with God, peace of conscience, and true comfort and happiness in Christ.

**28. ***{if ye loved me you would rejoice}* They loved him, but their love was not yet well informed concerning the issue of those things: because there was a dangerous mixture of self-love with their love to Christ, so were they most sorry for their own secular loss, in Christ’s departure from them, who still dreamed of a temporal kingdom of Christ.

*{greater than I}* Understand this, not of the Deity (for he is equal with God the Father) but as, to the office of a mediator, he took on him the form of a servant, Phil. 2:7. *Minor natus, non minoratus*, less born, not lessened in his birth, the Deity continuing one and the same, *Homo scilicet factus & permanens Deus*, becoming man, but continuing God. See Fulgentius *de f. orthodox.*; Alex. Alexandr. *init. Concil. Nicen. Bin. concil. tom.* 1; Hilar. *de trin.*, l. 9; Greg. Nazianzen. *orat.* 4; Basil, *contr. Eunom.*, l. 1; Augustin., l. 2, *de tradit. August.*, ep. 66, *Maximo*. He says this in respect of that nature, *quam nec exinanitus amisit*—which he lost not in his state of humiliation: *Homo enim assumptus est a Deo, non in homine consumptus est Deus*: ib. Aug. God assumed the manhood, but the manhood lessened not the Godhead.

**29. ***{that ye might believe}* That is, increase in faith, and be assured, that I am indeed the Son of the living God, and Savior of the elect (as Augustine expresses it) *Qui potuit hoc facere cum praedixisset, & praedicere, antequam hoc faceret*, who could do it when he had said it, and foretell it before he did it. See c. 13:19.

**30. ***{prince of this world}* The devil: as c. 12:31. He will stir up all the malignity of wicked men against me. See Luke 22:52. He will now in his last charge and onset muster up all his forces against me, and here again is a present put for a future, comes, that is, will very shortly come: as with many mischievous and malignant spirits with him, so with Judas, his guard, and the high priests’ servants, instigated by him, to destroy me; therein secure that he shall be conqueror; but herein deceived, that I shall destroy him and his works, not by force of arms, but by suffering, and dying for the redemption of the elect.

*{and hath nothing in me}* *Nihil juris in me*, no right nor power: that is, he shall not have his will in me, because he cannot tempt me to sin, though according to the holy will of my Father, I subject myself to death for man’s redemption.

**31. ***{but that the world may know}* As if he said, power is permitted to Satan and his accomplices to persecute me to death, that dying for man’s redemption, the world may see the obedience and love I bear to the Father, who has thus determined.

*{arise let us go hence}* They were yet sitting at table; now he hastens to meet and encounter with his passion: let us go to the place where the prince of darkness has prepared his armed troops to take and destroy me. See Matt. 26:46. Thus appeared his love to God the Father, whom he willingly obeyed to death, and to all the elect for whom he died.

## CHAPTER 15

**1. ***{I am the true vine}* By these manner of speeches he teaches, that being by the Father spiritually implanted into Christ, we have life by faith in him; and bring forth fruits of good works by virtue of his Spirit working in us; whereas we are by nature barren and unfruitful. Probably these speeches were occasioned by their passing through or by some vineyard; his usual manner being to take occasions of leading his hearers from the sight of things temporal, to the consideration of the spiritual. See c. 4:10, 32, 35; c. 6:27, &c. By true vine he means here, the most excellent, that which bears fruit for the elect of the whole world, at all seasons, in all ages.

**2. ***{every branch in me that beareth not fruit}* Every one in my church, (into which they are implanted by baptism, Rom. 6:3, 5 & 11:17, 20) that bears not good fruit, he prunes off, an unprofitable and dead branch, Matt. 3:10 and 15:13, to show that the regenerate have need of continual assistance and culture, without which they can do no good; he enlarges his allegory, to show God’s care of his vineyard, the church (Isa. 5:1, &c.) in pruning and correcting, to make it more fruitful: but for afflictions, men would grow high and barren; the minds of men are commonly luxuriant and barren in prosperity.

**3. ***{now ye}* Ch. 13:10. Lest they should be terrified by any fear, in the apprehension of their own infirmities and failings in temptations, as if they therefore were to be reputed unfruitful branches appointed to be cut off and cast into the fire, he comforts them with the assurance of their being cleared from their sins, *ye are already clean*. So the Syriac interprets this *ἤδη*, by *כדו* *jam nunc*, or *hucusque*, *already*, thus far; it only remains that you persevere.

*{are clean}* Sanctified by my Spirit. See 1 Cor. 6:11; 1 John 1:7.

*{word which I have spoken unto you}* Being accompanied by the power of my Spirit (see ch. 17:17; Eph. 5:26; 1 Pet. 1:22), he means the word of the Gospel, which they all believed, Judas excepted, ch. 13:11.

**4. ***{abide in me, and I in you}* Perseverance is a condition of salvation, Col. 1:23; Matt. 24:13. *Ab arbore frange ramum, fractus germinare non poterit*, break a bough from the tree, that which is broken off cannot bud forth, Cyprian. *tr.* 3, *de simpl. praelat.* Out of Christ there is no life of grace, no salvation. *Nec perveniet ad Christi praemia, qui relinquit Ecclesiam Christi*, neither shall he come to the rewards of Christ, who leaves the church of Christ: so dangerous is the state of a separatist.

**5. ***{without me ye do nothing}* Or *severed from me, you can do no good*: members divided from the head cannot live. You can do nothing truly good, but by me. See Hos. 14:8; Phil. 2:13. Are you afraid of taking away free will? *Operante—Spiritu Dei juvatur arbitrium, non aufertur: & hoc agit gratia ut voluntas peccato corrupta—non remaneat in languoribus suis, sed per opem miserantis medici curata revalescat*, &c. Ambros., l. 10, ep. 84. The Spirit of God working, the will is helped, not taken away: and grace works so, that the will corrupted by sin remains not in its sickness, but by the assistance of the merciful physician, being cured grows well again. To the like purpose says Prosper, *contr. collat.*, on that Matt. 16, *Blessed art thou Simon son of Jona for flesh and blood hath not revealed this unto thee, but my Father which is in heaven*. *Hac regula nulli hominum aufertur voluntas: quia virtus gratiae non hoc in voluntatibus operatur ut non sint; sed ut ex malis bonae*, &c. This rule takes not away any man’s will, because the power of grace does not work this in wills, that they should no more be, but that they should become good of evil; of unfaithful, faithful; and that they who were of themselves darkness, should become light in the Lord; that that which was dead, might be quickened, &c.

**6. ***{he is cast forth}* Out of the vineyard, as a branch cut off, &c., fit for nothing but the fire. See Matt. 3:10; Ezek. 15:2, 3, 4, &c. Here also appears the great danger of separation from Christ: there can be nothing but hell and damnation out of him. See on v. 4.

**7. ***{my words abide in you}* If you continue grounded and established in my doctrine.

*{ye shall ask what ye will, &c.}* *αἰτήσασθε*, that is, you may freely ask anything according to God’s will.

**8. ***{herein is my Father glorified}* See 5:16; Phil. 1:11; Eph. 1:12. Good works declare men the sons of God; as the fruits show the tree, as the effect the cause.

**9. ***{as the Father loveth me, &c.}* As the Father loves the Son and communicates all good to him, sustains him in his sufferings, and delivers him from death; so does Christ love all his; which is a singular ground of comfort to all those who love God: for first none can love him, but those whom he first loves, 1 John 4:19. Secondly, it is most certain, that the Father’s love to the Son, Christ, is eternal, and therefore unchangeable (he not only having love, as the creature in some weak measure may, but is love, 1 John 4:8, it is no accident, but his unchangeable essence: for *quicquid est in Deo, Deus est*) now Christ so loving us as his Father loves him, if we can be assured that he ever loved us, which by our love to him we may, we may be confident with Paul, Rom. 8:38, 39, that no creature shall be able to separate us from the love of God which is in Christ Jesus our Lord, because no creature can be able to separate Christ from the love of his Father.

*{so have I loved you}* I have loved you to the death. See 1 John 3:16. Giving myself for you, Eph. 5:2. Greater love can no man show, v. 13.

*{continue ye in my love}* Only this have I required for all my love to you, that you constantly love me. See Deut. 6:5 & 10:12. But how shall we continue in the love of Christ, who neither have any ability in ourselves to do any good, nor constancy to persevere in that we are moved to? He said v. 7, *ask what ye will*, &c. Whereas we must needs feel a want of the grace of perseverance; we ought to beg it of him, who only can give us to do that which he commands us. If any inquire concerning the rule, it follows in the next verse.

**10. ***{if ye keep my commandments}* 1 John 2:5. Specially that which he means v. 12. We can never love God if we love not our neighbors also. See 1 John 4:20. Keeping Christ’s commandments, imports a remembering them and laying them up in faithful memories, with resolution to do them.

**11. ***{that my joy may remain in you}* That is, the joy and comfort which you conceive in me your Mediator and Redeemer. See Rom. 14:17.

**12. ***{this is my commandment}* Ch. 13:34; 1 Thess. 4:9; 1 John 3:11. This is the sum of the second table, and the mark by which Christ will own his disciples. Without this all gifts are vain. See Matt. 22:37; ch. 13:35; 1 Cor. 13:1, &c. This is the end of the commandment, 1 Tim. 1:5.

**13. ***{greater love}* A man will rather lose his riches, and all that he has, than his life; better, (said the philosopher, when he cast his merchandise overboard in a storm) that these should perish for us, than with us: when a man therefore lays down his life for his friend, it appears that he loved him better than anything he had in this world.

**14. ***{ye are my friends if, &c.}* This was Abraham’s great privilege, James 2:23. But here is the condition of God’s holding friendship with us, if we do what he commands: principally if we love one another. See v. 17; 1 John 3:16. For sins comes the wrath of God upon the children of disobedience, Eph. 5:6. The just God can have no peace nor amity with willful contemners of his holy commandments; and the chief end and ground of all the commandments is love to God and man. See on v. 12 and 1 Tim. 1:5.

**15. ***{all things that I have heard of the Father}* All things necessary to salvation, see 2 Tim. 3:15.

**16. ***{ye have not chosen me}* He sets forth his love in the freedom thereof; it began not from any merit of theirs lest they should be proud. *Misericordia ejus praevenit eos secundum gratiam non secundum debitum*: Augustine *de praedest. sanct.*, lib. 1, cap. 17. His mercy prevented them according to (his) grace, not (their) merit, or debt. *Elegit ergo Deus fideles, sed ut sint, non quia jam erant*: ibid. Augustine. God therefore chose the faithful, but that they might be such, not because they were already such. God’s love is eternal, and therefore can have no cause but itself: for as Augustine says, *Non est—gratia, si praecesserint merita*; it is not grace (but debt) if merits preceded it. See Rom. 4:4. God made man at the beginning, not because he had any need of man or his service; but as Irenaeus, lib. 4, cap. 28, says well, *Ut haberet in quem collocaret sua beneficia*, that he might have one on whom he might confer his benefits; and therefore does he require man’s service, that because he is good and merciful, he might do good to them who persevere in his service, says the same.

*{that ye should bring forth fruit}* This is true in two senses. First, that election is the cause of our fruits of sanctification. See Rom. 8:30; 2 Pet. 1:10, and these fruits shall remain to eternity, Ps. 1:3; Matt. 25:34, &c.; Rev. 14:13. Secondly, that the disciples should go forth, and their preaching should have fruit and success to the conversion of the Gentiles, and that the church so planted by them should continue to the world’s end, in spite of all her enemies; the persecutions and all the machinations of Satan, because God gives the increase, 1 Cor. 3:7.

*{whatsoever ye shall ask}* Here he lays down a singular encouragement to ministers, in that they shall have free access to the Father to beg and obtain whatsoever they have need of in their ministry: there are indeed ever many lets, and enemies to our preaching the Gospel, whose assaults we are no ways able to bear, except the power and providence of God sustain us; therefore he says in effect, though you are insufficient, the Father will not be wanting to you.

**17. ***{that ye love one another}* This he says most seasonably to admonish the ministers of the Gospel to mutual love and concord: if there be strife of tongues, they must needs build to confusion: they ought to love the rather, because the world will ever hate them who love the truth. Love is the fulfilling of the law, no pretense will serve to the breach of this precept, seeing God has commanded mutual love: you will say, he is evil; and what are you who hate your brother? You say you hate the evil only; but malice is a false *medium*, making that seem evil which God accepts as good: but indeed, as Augustine said to Macedonius, ep. 54, *Omnes odit, qui malos odit*: he hates all, who hates the evil, seeing all men sin. But therefore *Diligendi sunt mali, ut non sint mali*: we must love the evil that they may not continue evil, as we love the sick to make them well.

**18. ***{If the world hate you}* That is, if the wicked hate you, as ch. 14:19, for such only hate Christ, and good men. Having admonished his disciples that they should abide in him, and love one another, he now shows them what they shall find contrary hereto, to wit an impious world, to draw them away from Christ, and from unity one with another, against which he fortifies them in the following words.

*{ye know that, &c.}* *γινώσκετε*, know you, or consider you; that you may be comforted in that therein you do but share with me in my sufferings.

**19. ***{therefore the world hateth you}* The wicked of the world (guided by the malicious spirit of Satan) hate the elect, for God’s favors bestowed on them. See Gen. 4:4, 5; 1 John 3:12. And because the image of God appears in their faith and sanctimony of life and conversation: he seems to correct others who dissents from them in judgment or practice: they think it strange that *you run not with them to the same excess of riot, speaking even of you*: 1 Pet. 4:4. The philosopher noted this impious quality of the heathenish world, in the example of Aristides hated, only because, *praeter modum justus erat*, he was more just than other men.

**20. ***{the servant is not greater, &c.}* Matt. 10:24; ch. 13:16.

*{if they have kept my saying}* That is, seeing they have not believed and obeyed my saying, but contemned it, it must not seem strange to you that they will also contemn and disobey your words. By this he will strengthen them against despair of success in their preaching, by his own example, be not out of heart when you perceive your auditors’ neglect and disrespect of the word you preach: all have not faith, and of rebellious Israel the apostle says, Heb. 4:2, *The word preached did not profit them, not being mixed with faith, in them that heard it*, and the same says, 2 Cor. 4:3, 4, *if our gospel be hid, it is hid to them that are lost: in whom the God of this world hath blinded the minds of them which believe not*, &c.

**21. ***{because they know not him that sent me}* They know not, or for malice will not acknowledge that which you preach to be the word of God. See Jer. 43:2 & 44:16. Ignorance of God is the cause of the persecution of his ministers.

**22. ***{if I had not come}* If I had not come unto the Jews according to the word of the prophets concerning me.

*{they had not had sin}* Either not so much sin, if they had not despised so clear light, and a truth so confirmed, as v. 24, or they should still have seemed innocent, if I had not discovered their hypocrisy. See ch. 9:41. And truly, *Minor erat culpa, ubi erat culpae ignorantia*, Ambrose, l. 9, ep. 71, there was less fault, where there was no knowledge of a fault. See Luke 12:48 & Matt. 10:15. But they that sin against knowledge, have no cloak or excuse for their sin.

**23. ***{hateth my Father}* For they are one God, and one truth, so that he that hates one, hates both.

**24. ***{not had sin}* Not so much sin: the more men sin against a convicted conscience, the greater and more desperate is their sin; see on v. 22.

**25. ***{written in their law}* Ps. 35:19. See ch. 10:34.

*{they hated me without a cause}* Undeservedly: for he did all things well, good to all, harm to none; so David’s enemies hated him, Ps. 15:19. *חנם* *chinnam*, 70. *οἱ* *μισοῦντές* *με* *δωρεὰν*, freely; that is, without any of my offense given them, hating me. So Jonathan said to his father King Saul, 1 Sam. 19:5, *why shouldest thou sin against innocent blood* *חנם*, *δωρεὰν* undeservedly.

**26. ***{when the Comforter is come}* Ch. 14:26; Luke 24:49.

*{whom I will send}* So the Holy Ghost is said to be sent of the Father (ch. 14:26) and the Son, of the Father and Holy Ghost, Isa. 61:1; Luke 4:18, not by change of place, but *illo missionis nomine*, under that name of mission, we must understand, *opus manifestationis indulta*, the work of manifestation indulged, as Fulgentius speaks, *de miss. Sp. S. ad Monim.*, l. 2, and as Greg. *hom.* 26, *in Evang.*, says well, *ejus missio, ipsa processio est*, his mission, is his proceeding of the Father and the Son, he is coequal, consubstantial, coeternal with the Father and the Son: for as Aug. *de temp. serm.* 184 says, in the holy Trinity is nothing, *minus aut majus*, lesser or greater. He came down on his apostles, in several gifts (as the same says, *serm.* 185) *velut imber sanctificationis illapsus*—falling, as it were a shower of sanctification. How great, how unspeakable is the goodness of our Redeemer? *Hominem portavit ad caelum, & Deum misit ad terras*: ib. He carried man to heaven (as in his ascending humanity) and sent God (the Holy Ghost) unto the earth. See also Hilar. *de trin.*, l. 8.

**27. ***{ye also bear witness}* You who were eyewitnesses, who have heard and seen the very same which the Spirit also testifies. Acts 1:8, 21, 22 & 5:32; 1 John 1:1, 2, 3.

## CHAPTER 16

**1. ***{Should not be offended}* *σκανδαλισθῆτε*, comes of *σκάζω*, to halt; *σκάνδαλον*, of the same, is a stumbling-block, or stone in the way, at which, suddenly striking the foot we fall or stumble. See Rom. 14:13; 1 Pet. 2:8, in both which places it is interpreted by *πρόσκομμα*, *offendiculum*, of *προσκόπτω*, *impingo*, to dash one’s foot against something in the way. Here Christ tells them beforehand, what troubles they must look for in preaching the Gospel; lest stumbling at that which they expected not, they might fall from their patience, hope in, and profession of Christ; flesh and blood (ever too indulgent to itself) being apt to suggest to them in their persecutions and continual troubles, that God favored not the preaching and doctrine of the Gospel, which all the world disliked and persecuted, and for which such grievous things were inflicted on the preachers and professors thereof: therefore Christ forearms them by forewarning them what they must expect: for that which suddenly falls upon one, may trouble a great and constant mind, but that which is foreseen and expected, is more easily borne, as Cyril notes. But herein is a singular argument of the truth and sincerity of Christian religion; that it could ever take root, and so many generations, according to the word of Christ, Matt. 16:19, in spite of the gates of hell, and powers of darkness, it yet flourishes; though it has been so constantly persecuted in all times and places; and seeing that Christ, at the first proposal thereof, never allured any one disciple to follow him by promise of honors, pleasures, secular gains, or anything which flesh and blood likes; but on the contrary told them plainly, as here, so, many other times, that they must expect persecutions, malice of all the world, loss of goods, and life itself for professing the same: he that on these terms and conditions is a resolved Christian, must, by the Spirit of truth, be ascertained of the truth of his religion, otherwise he would never suffer such things for the profession thereof; the very spectators of the martyrs’ sufferings (when they have seen them torn with sundry torments, and under the tired hands of their tormentors, hold an invincible patience) have thought, as the truth is, that neither the consent of so many, nor the perseverance of dying men could be vain, *nec ipsam patientiam, sine Deo, cruciatus tantos posse superare*, Lactant., lib. 5, cap. 13, neither that patience itself, without God, could overcome such great torments: hence came it, that the blood of the martyrs was the seed of the Gospel.

**2. ***{put you out of the synagogues}* Excommunicate you. See ch. 9:22.

*{whosoever killeth you will think}* They shall in their blind zeal think you such pernicious and evil men, that you are worthy to die, as was commanded concerning the idolaters, Exod. 32:27, and blasphemers, Lev. 24:16. See Acts 22:22.

**3. ***{because they have not known the Father}* See ch. 15:21. Ignorance of God, is the cause of persecutors’ rage: so unhappy a mother of devotion is ignorance, this made them persecute Christ in his sacred person, Acts 3:17, and in his servants, Acts 9:5; 1 Tim. 1:13.

**4. ***{I said unto you at the beginning}* He would not at first so expressly tell them of all these things which they must suffer, but considered their weakness: and indeed while he was with them, the rage of the adversaries fell upon him; but after his ascension, when Satan could no more reach him, he stirred up all the fury of wicked men against the professors of the truth of Christ: it was therefore necessary, both that now before his departure, they should be forewarned of their dangers; and after his ascension, that they should be armed with a greater measure of the Holy Ghost, as to instruct them in all truth, so to confirm them in their professing and preaching the same, which was also done accordingly.

*{because I was with you}* He speaks as if he were not now with them, because he was upon his departure from them. And we may hence learn, that as Christ not at once declares all the mysteries of eternal life and salvation unto his hearers, but accommodates his doctrine to their capacities, (as v. 12; 1 Cor. 3:1, 2; Heb. 5:11, 12) so does he in due time and measure instruct them therein, that the believers may be saved.

**5. ***{and none of you asketh me}* This is not simply to be understood, for Peter asked it, ch. 13:36, but he greatly reproves their negligence, for that they now no more cared to be further informed what should be the effect of his departure.

**6. ***{sorrow hath filled your hearts}* Because I tell you now of my departure, and your afflictions to come: whereas if you would have diligently inquired concerning the end of these things, you should have found matter of rejoicing in the assurance of my glory, and the accomplishment of your redemption therein.

**7. ***{it is expedient for you}* It shall make for your advantage. Such are the works of God’s power and unsearchable counsel, whereby he brings light out of darkness, good out of evil; and comfort out of sorrow: so he gives them his peace in the world’s malice, and spiritual joy of the Holy Ghost in the bitter sorrows and afflictions of flesh and blood: so he advantages them in manifesting to themselves, and the world, their righteousness, by permitting them to be condemned of the world for malefactors: so he gives them life, by permitting them to death: so tries he the elect, and blinds the carnal-hearted.

*{if I go not away, the Comforter, &c.}* Could he not have given the Holy Ghost being present with them? Yes, but God had otherwise determined to confirm the truth of the Gospel, by the manifest graces of the Holy Ghost, and them therein, who were now to be weaned from the desire of Christ’s bodily presence, and to awaked out of their dream of a temporal and worldly kingdom of Christ. See 2 Cor. 5:16.

**8. ***{he will reprove the world of sin}* *ἐλέγξει*, he will not only accuse but also convince the world of sin, by such evident and concluding arguments, as that *πᾶσαν* *ἀπολογίαν* *αὐτῶν* *ἐκκόψει*, Chrysost. *hom.* 77. He shall take from them all excuse, he shall convince their obstinacy and unbelief, by the power of truth making it clearly appear, that I am the Christ. See Acts 2:37.

**9. ***{of sin}* Of unbelief: see ch. 15:22, 24. However the world and secure sinners think themselves free from the guilt of sin and the just punishment thereto due, yet when by the light of God’s Spirit they shall understand their own condition under the burden of original sin, derived to them from Adam, and their many actual, contracted by their own willful disobedience, they shall so be convinced of sin, as that they shall groan under the burden thereof as unsupportable, until they by faith can lay hold on Christ for their peace with, and justification before God. See Rom. 8:23; Rom. 7:23, 24, 25; Rom. 5:1. Thus shall the light of the Holy Ghost reprove the world of sin, thereby moving the consciences of the elect to faith and repentance, and terrifying the hearts of the reprobates, who can neither find peace to their souls under the curse of the law, nor believe in Christ that they might be delivered from it.

*{because they believe not}* And therefore are still liable to the power and curse of the law against sinners, so long as they continue in unbelief, being not made free by the Son, who only freely justifies sinners. See ch. 8:34, 35, 36; Rom. 3:19, 24 & 5:1, &c.

**10. ***{of righteousness}* Not the world’s, but Christ’s: because they contemned the true righteousness of God in Christ (1 Cor. 1:30) whom they blasphemed, and condemned as a malefactor: hereof their consciences shall be convinced, when they shall perceive the Gospel confirmed, by the admirable power of the Holy Ghost, evidently working by the apostles.

**11. ***{of judgment}* Because they condemned him whom God the Father justified (which was not his injustice, but theirs) and appointed Judge of the living and the dead, Acts 17:31, or my power of judicature exercised against Satan in part already, ch. 12:31; Luke 10:18, &c.

*{prince of this world}* Satan; not that he is so; for God alone is King, and his providence rules over every creature but because he arrogates to himself that high title (Luke 4:6) lying to deceive, and because the wicked are permitted to his government, 2 Cor. 4:4. Of this see Acts 2:22, &c., that they might perish through his delusions who received not the love of the truth that they might be saved. 2 Thess. 2:11, 12.

*{is judged}* Shall presently be judged, that is, overcome, and his kingdom overthrown by Christ’s passion, and the preaching thereof. See Heb. 2:14; Luke 11:22 & 10:18.

**12. ***{I have yet many things to say unto you}* Thus he attempers to his hearers’ capacity: his doctrine distills like rain upon the herbs, (Deut. 32:2) by little and little as the hearers can receive it to nourish them, not by cataracts to drown them. See 1 Cor. 3:1. I have many deep mysteries, which you cannot now understand, or bear them without offense by reason of your weakness, but the Holy Ghost will reveal them unto you: see John 6:56, 60; 1 Cor. 3:1. See on v. 4.

**13. ***{he will shew you things to come}* Concerning the kingdom of Christ, which they knew not till after his resurrection.

**14. ***{he shall receive of mine}* He shall show you the same truth which I have spoken to you, opening the treasure of wisdom and sanctification, which are yet hid in me: he shall fill you with my Spirit, and make you understand that which I have taught you, sealing you up to the redemption which I work for you, and assuring you of your interest in, and conjunction with me.

**15. ***{all things that the Father hath are mine}* This he speaks in the person of the Mediator; because of his fullness we all receive, ch. 1:16, and therein shows the unity of essence in the holy Trinity, and community of power, wisdom, sanctity, truth, eternity, glory, majesty, &c.

**16. ***{because I go to the Father}* See ch. 14:18, 19. I pass by death and resurrection into the glory which I had, and have eternally with the Father; yet shall I be more spiritually with you than ever; because you shall then have more experience of my Spirit. See Matt. 28:20.

**17. ***{Then said some of his disciples}* Often had he mentioned his death, and going to the Father, yet understood they not; so deaf is flesh and blood to the doctrine of the cross; and so blind are we until the Spirit of God illuminate us, 1 Cor. 2:14.

**18. ***{what is this that he saith}* What means he hereby? They understood not, that he should so soon die, and so quickly rise again from the dead, the third day: therefore that same *τὸ* *μικρὸν*, *a little while*, was obscure and hard to them, until this prophecy opened itself in the fulfilling thereof. There is in the regenerate in this life, so great a remainder of carnal reason and wisdom, which is but spiritual blindness, as that they understand but in part (see Gal. 5:17; 1 Cor. 13:9) even sometimes plain and easy things often taught them: as in this example appears he had often, and but lately acquainted them with his death, and suffering at Jerusalem, and his rising again the third day, yet they thought not that it had been so nigh, that he should that very night be apprehended, and slain the next day; they understood nothing of these things, *and this saying was hid from them*, &c., Luke 18:31, 32, 33, 34.

**19. ***{that they were desirous to ask him}* They, like good auditors, are not offended at that they do not presently understand, but resolve to ask that they may learn: he as a gracious Savior, prevents their inquiry by a further explanation of himself.

**20. ***{ye shall weep, &c.}* You shall have sorrow and trouble of heart; when impiety and popular rage shall triumph: but you shall have a spiritual joy in receiving the Holy Ghost; which shall take away, and make void secular and worldly sorrows (as Aaron’s serpent did the enchanters’, Exod. 7:12), so had they many sharp encounters, injuries, pressures, sorrows as Christ foretold them, Matt. 9:15, &c., but they rejoiced that they were counted worthy to suffer for the name of Christ: so are the saints advantaged in their sufferings. See Acts 5:41; 2 Cor. 4:16, 17, 18; Rom. 5:3. When the saints weep, the world shall rejoice, but when all tears shall be wiped from their eyes, (Rev. 7:17) then *shall all the kindreds of the earth wail and mourn*, Matt. 24:30; Rev. 1:7. So do the saints and the world take their turns of sorrows and tears, as Gideon’s fleece and floor did of the dew. Judg. 6:37, &c.

**21. ***{her hour is come}* The time of her travail appointed of God. This similitude often used in holy Scripture, imports sudden fear and sorrow. Ps. 48:6; Jer. 6:24; Jer. 13:21 & 22:23 & 49:24 & 50:43; Mic. 4:9, 10; 1 Thess. 5:3. So should their dispersion, fear and sorrow be, but they should (and so did) rejoice when the issue thereof, the resurrection, and ascension of Christ, the gifts and graces of the Holy Ghost poured out upon them, and their admirable comfort in their faith’s confirmation, as also in the conversion of the believers of Jews and Gentiles to the faith, and their salvation by Christ.

**22. ***{I will see you again}* After my resurrection from the dead.

*{no man taketh from you}* *αἴρει*, *taketh*, *ἀρεῖ*, *shall be able to take away*: because Christ is their joy (says Augustine) who shall die no more, but be to the fountain and author of eternal joy. See 1 Pet. 1:8. This joy is begun in this life, and completed in the life to come.

**23. ***{in that day ye shall ask me nothing}* When you shall see me risen from the dead; you shall not then inquire of me those things which you now doubt of; as ch. 13:36 & 14:5. You shall be taught of the Holy Ghost, and so understand these things, 1 John 2:27, or you shall not ask of me as now you do.

**24. ***{ye asked nothing in my name}* Hitherto you have not grounded your requests to God through my mediation and intercession, which you shall now learn to do in my bodily absence. Hereby he comforted them in assurance of his spiritual assistance of them in all their trials, which they should obtain in their faithful and earnest prayers unto God in his name and mediation only; which they according to this word of Christ doing, had a comfortable experience of the performance of this his promise on that behalf; whereof see Acts 4:24, 29, 30, 31, 32, &c. This was probably an unusual manner of praying with the former saints before the coming of Christ; for the apostles had not yet practiced it after his manifestation to Israel, and their being so much instructed by and under his divine magistery, until after his ascension into heaven; to wit, their praying to God through the name and mediation of Christ.

**25. ***{in proverbs}* Or *parables*, in similitudes, in dark sayings, but openly, as v. 29 & ch. 10:24 & ch. 11:10; Mark 8:32.

*{no more speak unto you}* I will henceforth clearly enlighten you.

**26. ***{Ye shall ask in my name}* Ch. 14:13, 14, for my sake, through my mediation, and according to the will and promise of God: otherwise the name of Christ is but vainly pretended, *Nam si id quod non expedit petitur, non in nomine Jesu petitur pater*, Gregor. *hom.* 26. For if that which is not expedient is asked, the Father is not entreated in the name of Jesus.

*{I say not unto you}* Not that he will not mediate: but to show the readiness of God’s love to hear the believers.

**27. ***{because ye have loved me}* This *ὅτι* *because*, here as Luke 7:47, shows a consequence not a cause of God’s love to believers. See on Luke 7:47. We love Christ, because God loved us first, which love of his can have no cause but itself: our love to Christ is therefore a certain sign of God’s love to us.

**28. ***{I leave the world}* In respect of my bodily presence; but as his Deity left not heaven to come into the world by his incarnation; so neither left it the world by going to the Father in his assumption.

**29. ***{speakest no proverb}* No parable, or obscure thing; now we better perceive what you meant. They found in themselves some increase of illumination, so that they could truly say Christ’s present words were not now such obscure riddles, but that they in some measure understood the scope and meaning thereof; but herein they yet failed, that they thought they were sufficiently informed, or knew enough concerning his going to his Father, with the effects thereof, much more clearly and evidently taught them after his ascension, and the coming of the Holy Ghost upon them, as he had promised; when we know most, we must know that we are yet to learn, as God shall be pleased further to reveal himself unto us.

**30. ***{Now we are sure}* This they say as men fluctuant between fear and hope; therefore Christ presently tells them of that effect of their faith’s weakness which he foresaw, that is, their forsaking him, whom they here profess came from God; so infirm is the strongest faith, so inconstant the best resolutions.

**31. ***{Do ye now believe?}* This interrogation has in it the significations both of pity and reproof of their too early confidence in themselves, as if he said, Ah poor souls! Who seem to yourselves thus strong in faith, you know not how quickly you shall be scattered for fear. See v. 32.

**32. ***{Behold the hour cometh}* Matt. 26:31; Mark 14:27.

*{yet am I not alone}* This also he adds to confirm them against their trial now at hand: as if he said, let not go your confidence in me, when you shall find your own infirmity in your forsaking me for fear, God will still be with me, when men desert me.

**33. ***{that in me ye might, &c.}* That trusting in me, you may have peace of conscience, the surest armor against secular afflictions.

*{I have overcome the world}* Christ’s victory is our victory. See 1 John 4:4. Neither the world, Satan, sin, death, nor any creature could overcome him; so neither shall they us, Rom. 8:37, &c., though we must through many afflictions enter into the kingdom of heaven, Acts 14:22 (for as Jerome, lib. 2, ep. 19, says, *quis sanctorum sine certamine coronatus est?* Which of all the saints has been crowned without a conflict?), yet herein is our comfort and assurance, we have overcome the world, because Christ has overcome it: this is the victory of our faith; we have overcome, though the fight be not yet done; because we are certain of victory in Christ, who has overcome for us, both the world and whatsoever is adverse to our salvation: the world may still afflict us and try us, to advantage us, not finally to hurt us, because we are in Christ by faith. See 1 John 5:9; Rom. 8:1.

## CHAPTER 17

**1. ***{The hour is come}* This he says, because though Christ were manifested, by doctrine and miracles, power and great works; yet his spiritual kingdom, which after this, more clearly shined forth, was yet but obscure towards the world: now the hour which the Father had appointed was come.

*{glorify thy Son}* He prays for that he was assured of: assurance of the truth of God’s promises is the cause and ground of faithful prayer, 2 Cor. 4:13. So we daily pray for remission of sins, because we are assured that they are remitted in Christ: God has appointed that we shall attain through the merit and mediation of Christ, but the condition on our part to be performed is, that we ask like dutiful children, that he may give like a loving Father.

*{that thy Son may also glorify thee}* That I may glorify you in the afflictions which I shall suffer for the elect, that men may know that I loved you. Euthym.

**2. ***{as thou hast given him power}* Authority over all men, and all creatures.

**3. ***{the only true God}* Not excluding himself and the Holy Ghost; but intimating the whole Deity in the name of God.

**4. ***{I have glorified thee}* By doctrine and miracles; by my whole life on earth: now therefore glorify me in my death and resurrection, that you may also therein be glorified. See ch. 5:23.

**5. ***{with the glory which I had, &c.}* That the divine majesty which I had from all eternity may now shine forth in the person of the Mediator; for the nature of the flesh was not yet glorified, and taken up to God’s right hand, as Chrysostom notes: this glory was (towards the world) clouded for a time by the veil of his flesh, in the form of a servant, but nothing in himself lessened thereby.

**6. ***{unto the men}* To my disciples chosen out of the world, whom I have instructed. He preached to all the Jews publicly in their synagogues, and in the temple, as occasion served; even all that concerned their believing in, and salvation by him; but there were among them all, but a certain number of the elect who received that word, and believed to salvation.

*{thine they were}* By eternal election. They were God’s, that is, the Father’s, the Son’s, and the Holy Ghost’s, who gave and committed them to Christ as he was their Redeemer, Teacher, Savior, and Mediator; not alienating them by that donation; but more nearly uniting them to God by that mediation, whereby they become, *θείας* *κοινωνοὶ* *φύσεως*, *partakers of the divine nature*. God gave them to the magistery of Christ, that in the days of his flesh, and conversation with them, he might fully teach them those things, which they were, after his ascension into heaven, to teach all nations, that the elect among them might believe in him and be saved.

**7. ***{are of thee}* The elect believing know, that all that which they have in Christ is divine and heavenly, no invention or power of man, but the will and power of God to salvation to every one that believes, Rom. 1:16. See ch. 6:69; 1 John 5:20.

**8. ***{and have known}* Ch. 16:27. For faith cannot be without knowledge. See ch. 9:36; Rom. 10:14. Faith is the assent of the mind unto that it knows.

**9. ***{I pray for them}* Specially for the apostles designed to so great and dangerous a work, as was their preaching the Gospel to the obstinate Jews and idolatrous Gentiles. I pray not for the reprobates of the world. Christ intercedes for the elect only who receive his word to faith and obedience; he neither prays for, nor hears their prayers who will not hear, and obey his word and ordinance, see Zech. 7:11, 13; Isa. 1:15.

**10. ***{thine are mine}* For God the Father made him heir of all things, Heb. 1:2. This he says to give them assurance that the Father heard him; as if he had said, I commend none unto you, but those whom you gave me, and still own for yours.

*{I am glorified in them}* As in their believing and sanctification, so in that they shall preach my triumphs over sin, the cross, death, and hell, to all the world, out of which believers shall continually be gathered.

**11. ***{I am no more in the world}* I am presently to depart from this world, and after my ascension will be no more in it by my bodily presence (Acts 3:21); by his governing and assisting Spirit, he ever is. See Matt. 28:20.

*{keep through thine own name}* That is (as the interpreter of the Syriac has it) *by thy name*; by your power, mercy, and goodness; for your holy name’s sake, or by the knowledge of you: because they are yours, and bear your name: *διὰ* *τῆς* *σῆς* *βοηθείας*, *through your help, or assistance*. Chrysost.

*{that they may be one}* Joined in unity of faith and love as brethren in me. Of one heart, mind and consent, careful to keep the unity of the Spirit in the bond of peace. Eph. 4:3. The effect of Christ’s prayer concerning this, admirably appeared, Acts 4:32; Acts 1:14 & 2:46 & 5:12 & 15:25. Lord Jesus Christ, yesterday, today, and the same for ever; now remember and intercede for your distracted church, whose discordant parts like those dry bones in the prophet’s vision, lie scattered, O Lord God of peace and unity, breathe the spirit of unity upon them, that they may come together and live. Amen Lord Jesus.

**12. ***{son of perdition}* This is an Hebraism. So 2 Thess. 2:3, *ὁ* *υἱὸς* *τῆς* *ἀπωλείας*, *that son of perdition*. Eph. 2:3, *τέκνα* *ὀργῆς*, children of wrath. Judas was a son of destruction; or who deserved destruction; destinated to destruction: not that Judas was without sin (because designed to perdition) or that God was the author of sin; no, no: Fulgentius *de praed. ad Monim.*, lib. 1, says very well, *quos praedestinavit ad poenam, non praedestinavit* *ad culpam*; those whom he predestinated to punishment, he predestinated not to sin; and therefore, *illius rei Deus ultor est, cujus author non est*, ib. God is the revenger of that, whereof he is not the author, that is, iniquity, which he can punish, but not do: there is no sin in him, and therefore none can be of him. The Hebrews used to say, *בן* *מות* *ben maveth*, 1 Sam. 20:31 and 26:16; 2 Sam. 12:5, &c., a son of death, that is, one desperately perishing, notoriously wicked. So they said, a son of Belial, Deut. 13:13; 1 Sam. 2:12, without profit, lawless, rebellious, given over to wickedness. Judg. 19:22; 1 Kings 21:10; 1 Sam. 25:25.

*{that the Scripture, &c.}* Ps. 109:8. God foreseeing his treason and impenitency, foretold of his destruction, and pronounced many severe and grievous curses and plagues to come upon that traitor, Ps. 69:22, 23, &c.; Ps. 109:7, &c., as on all other impious and unreconcilable enemies of the church of God.

**13. ***{I speak in the world}* That is, being yet bodily present, I pray for and with them.

*{my joy fulfilled}* Ch. 15:11. By (*in themselves*) he means in their hearts; and in their comfortable assurance in this life, and accomplished salvation in the life to come; which he duly calls his, who rejoices in our good, and causes the same in us, by the presence of his Spirit, sanctifying us, and living in us, Gal. 2:20. Whereas we have of ourselves nothing but unrest, terror, and matter of despair; Christ only is our peace, and joy.

**14. ***{they are not of the world}* Because being separated by the spirit of regeneration, they dissent from it in affection and conversation, 1 Pet. 4:4.

**15. ***{take them out of the world}* By present death, because it was appointed that they should preach the Gospel to the nations of the world.

**16. ***{they are not of the world}* Verse 14. This he repeats for their sakes, that they might remember that they were but aliens here; and so no wonder at the world hated them, which loves only her own.

**17. ***{sanctify them through thy truth}* Consecrate, prepare, and enable them to preach your truth to the world; as if he said, let them first receive the roll and digest it; to wit, the word: which they must preach to others. See Ezek. 3:1, 2, 3, 4; Rev. 10:9, 10, 11.

*{thy word is the truth}* Thus he meets with their hypocrisy, who boast much of truth and godliness, yet securely contemn or neglect the word of God; whereas, that only contains the truth and will of God, for our sanctification and salvation: all other truths pretended to, or alleged by men, are hereby to be examined. Aaron and his sons were sanctified to the ministration of the Levitical priesthood, with cleansings and sprinkling the blood of those sacrifices (Exod. 29:7) which could never make them perfect (Heb. 9:9), but, O my Father, sanctify and consecrate these in the truth of those shadows (now to be antiquated and done away, Eph. 5:26; 1 Pet. 3:18; John 10:15 & 15:3; 1 Pet. 1:22) which more clearly shines forth in the Gospel, than in those figures of the law.

**18. ***{as thou hast sent me}* You have sent me to preach the Gospel, your will for man’s salvation, I now having finished my course, assign them to the same office, and assist and keep you them, that they may perform it.

**19. ***{for their sakes}* That I may for them merit remission of sins, and eternal life: that they may also be sanctified and consecrated to you and your service, as I am to do your will for the expiation of the sin of man: he seems to set himself against the Levitical sacrifices and priesthood which prefigured him, and were thereto consecrated and sanctified, Exod. 29:1, those in the shadow, Heb. 10:1, but Christ was so consecrated in the truth, ch. 1:17; Acts 10:38; Luke 4:18; Acts 10:38; Isa. 61:1. So were the apostles anointed, according to their measure to preach *the acceptable year*; but not as Christ, who received the Spirit, but not by measure, John 3:34, but the fullness of the Deity, Col. 2:9. The sum of this petition, is, that God would be pleased to sanctify the apostles, and prepare them to preach the Gospel to all nations, as he had sanctified and appointed him to declare the same unto them: intimating that as all good in order to man’s salvation was, and is derived from his death and the power of his resurrection from the dead, so also all abilities and consecration of the ministers of Christ, subordinate to the same.

*{I sanctify myself}* That is, I consecrate and voluntary offer up myself an holy and unblemished sacrifice to you for their redemption. So Euthymius, after Chrysostom, interprets.

*{might be sanctified}* Through the imputation of my righteousness to them; in that I have been obedient to the death for them. See Heb. 10:10, as also for the rest of your elect, as v. 20.

*{through thy truth}* *ἐν* *ἀληθείᾳ*, *in the truth*, or truly, and so it intimates our true and real sanctification by the one oblation of Christ, in opposition to those cleansings under the law by the offering up of beasts in sacrifice.

**20. ***{for these alone}* That is, the apostles now with me; but for all the elect which shall hereafter believe. Here was comfort to the apostles in that many should believe through their ministry; so is there to all true believers; in that Christ (whose prayer and intercession preserved Peter’s faith from falling in his dangerous trial) has prayed for us also. God will deny him nothing.

**21. ***{that they may all be one}* Members of one body of Christ, whose unity is life.

*{in us}* Not one in evil, sin, malice; but in faith, charity, sanctity, consent in good, holding the unity of the Spirit in the bond of peace. Sin separates man from God; Christ can have no communion with Belial, unhappy sinner. *Magna hominis miseria est cum illo non esse, sine quo non potest esse*. Prosper. *Epigram.* It is a man’s great misery not to be with him, without whom he cannot be (happy).

*{that the world may believe}* Being convicted by their holy unity, may confess the power of my Spirit in them, and be united to their society of faith and holy obedience.

**22. ***{and the glory}* That is, Christian unity, which is the honor of God, the gift of Christ, and the glory of true Christians: or the heavenly felicity whereof the elect shall participate in Christ.

**23. ***{I in them}* That the life of my Spirit may appear in their holy conversation, as Gal. 2:20.

*{thou in me}* By your divine nature united to my person; and communicating the fullness of your Spirit to me.
